Kept in sync by integrateBodies().\nstruct PosSoA {\n    std::vector<double> x, y, z;\n};\n\n// Number of bodies i processed together; each lane accumulates its own force\n// over j in the original sequential order, so results are bitwise identical.\nconstexpr size_t IBLOCK = 8;\n\nstatic inline void forceBlock(const PosSoA& p, std::vector<Body>& bodies, size_t i0, size_t n) {\n    const double* __restrict px = p.x.data();\n    const double* __restrict py = p.y.data();\n    const double* __restrict pz = p.z.data();\n\n    if (i0 + IBLOCK <= n) {\n        double xi[IBLOCK], yi[IBLOCK], zi[IBLOCK];\n        double Fx[IBLOCK], Fy[IBLOCK], Fz[IBLOCK];\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < IBLOCK; ++k) {\n            xi[k] = px[i0 + k];\n            yi[k] = py[i0 + k];\n            zi[k] = pz[i0 + k];\n            Fx[k] = 0.0;\n            Fy[k] = 0.0;\n            Fz[k] = 0.0;\n        }\n        for (size_t j = 0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfjYaYjHWbzXbqTQx7Gdv","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I parallelized `nbody/nbody.cpp` with OpenMP, and the final positions and velocities are bit-for-bit identical to the original. I checked the output hash against the original binary at n = 1, 7, 1000, 1024, 3001, 4099 and 8192, including runs with 1 to 256 threads.\n\n**Speed (n=8192, 10 steps):**\n\n| Version | Time |\n|---|---|\n| Original | 4974 ms |\n| Parallel, 1 thread | ~1240 ms (4× faster from vectorization alone) |\n| Parallel, 128 threads | 22–32 ms (~200×) |\n\nAt n=32768 it goes from 80 s on 1 thread to 5.06 s on 16 threads and 1.28 s on 64. That's close to linear scaling; the 1-thread figure is from a build that wasn't vectorizing properly yet. On the final build, n=32768 runs in about 0.18 s on 128 threads.\n\n**Changes in `nbody.cpp`:**\n- **One parallel region for the whole simulation.** Threads are started once, not every step. Each step has two shared loops: forces (updates velocities) and integration (updates positions). The barrier after the force loop ensures every force is computed from the previous step's positions, as in the original.\n- **Positions kept in three separate arrays (x, y, z).** This lets the CPU process several bodies at once with SIMD instructions. The force kernel handles 8 bodies at a time, and each body still adds up its forces over j in the original order. That's why the results are exact. A fallback loop handles any leftover bodies when n isn't a multiple of 8.\n- **The energy calculation (only used with `-v`) is also parallel.** Because the additions happen in a different order, the printed final energy differs in the last few digits (438012510.756800 vs 438012510.756872, a relative difference of about 1.6e-13). The simulation results themselves are unaffected.\n\n**Changes in `CMakeLists.txt`:**\n- Added `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` and linked OpenMP, so the program always uses it.\n- Added `-fno-math-errno`. Without it, GCC won't vectorize the `sqrt` loop because `sqrt` might set `errno`. It can't change any value here, since the square root's argument is always at least SOFTENING > 0.\n- Added `-ffp-contract=off`. The original's results depend on exactly which multiply-adds GCC fuses into single FMA instructions. I read the original's assembly and wrote those fusions out as explicit `std::fma` calls, so the compiler can't fuse differently and change the results.\n\n**Thread count:** by default it uses all 256 logical CPUs, but 128 threads (one per physical core) is faster. On n=32768, 128 threads took about 0.18 s against 0.23–0.27 s for 256. The kernel is limited by the square-root/divide unit, which two hyperthreads on the same core have to share. For best results, run with `OMP_NUM_THREADS=<physical cores>`; I didn't hardcode a thread count."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":771,"cache_read_input_tokens":42831,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":771},"output_tokens":6,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"33ddd2bd-ef1b-441b-b62a-abfea344ea64","uuid":"c977c18d-9cb8-44ee-ac8e-7c253da3076a","timestamp":"2026-10-05T18:14:40.322Z","request_id":"req_011CfjYaYE22KQS7H574U5mK"}
